## 🤖 Claude *(18:15:03)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I need to double-check the supabase-js v2 API for handling duplicate key conflicts on inserts—specifically whether `ignoreDuplicates` is available on `.insert()` or if it's only on `.upsert()`.  The key issue is that I'm using a partial unique index with a `WHERE status <> 'failed'` condition, which means Postgres needs the predicate included in the `ON CONFLICT` clause to properly match the index, not just the column name alone.

Supabase's upsert method doesn't support specifying that partial index predicate, so I can't express `ON CONFLICT (idempotency_key) WHERE status <> 'failed' DO NOTHING` through the SDK — I'd need raw SQL for that. I'm weighing whether to write a custom RPC function or reconsider the index strategy.

Actually, there's a simpler path: I can just do a plain `.insert()` without any ON CONFLICT logic. The partial unique index will still enforce uniqueness at insert time, so if a duplicate key arrives with `status <> 'failed'`, Postgres rejects it with error code 23505. I catch that error and treat it as idempotent — the record was already dispatched, so no action needed. This leverages the index's natural constraint enforcement without needing ON CONFLICT syntax at all. Fixing the error handling block to properly reflect the idempotent behavior where a duplicate insert attempt becomes a no-op, and verifying that `.maybeSingle()` is the right choice for the insert operation.

</details>


## 🤖 Claude *(18:15:04)*

I need to fix the idempotency-claim API: the migration created a **partial** unique index (`WHERE status <> 'failed'`), which supabase-js `.upsert({onConflict})` cannot target (Postgres can't infer a partial index without its predicate). The faithful pattern is a plain `.insert()` that lets the partial index reject the duplicate with SQLSTATE 23505 — identical effect to `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`.

A prior FAILED row does NOT block (predicate releases the key = retry).\n        const { data: claim, error: claimErr } = await admin\n          .from(\"cadence_dispatches\")\n          .insert({\n            user_id: userId, plan_id: planId, step_index: stepIndex, occurrence_at: occurrenceAt.toISOString(),\n            idempotency_key: idempotencyKey, channel, status: \"queued\",\n          })\n          .select(\"id\").maybeSingle();\n        if (claimErr) {\n          if (claimErr.code === \"23505\") { results.push({ channel, status: \"already_dispatched\" }); continue; }  // NO-OP\n          throw claimErr;\n        }\n        if (!claim?.id) { results.push({ channel, status: \"already_dispatched\" }); continue; }\n        const dispatchId = claim.id as string;"

Both type-check clean. Done.

**Path:** `/home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/supabase/functions/cadence-run/index.ts` (novo; `deno check` exit 0)

**3 linhas:**
- `cadence-run` é cron-only (gate `Authorization === Bearer SB_SECRET_KEY` → 403 in-function), drena o plano por `plan_id`, deriva `userId` **da linha** `autopilot_plans.user_id` (nunca do body), resolve o subject (`creative_assets` owner-scoped), roda o gate chain server-side (quiet_hours no fuso do sujeito → HALT · frequency_cap lendo `channel_profiles.cadence` → HALT · budget_cap_mco · digest dia), enfileira em `scheduled_posts` e re-arma `next_run_at` em UTC.
- Idempotência: claim **ledger-first** em `cadence_dispatches` (plain `.insert()` que deixa o **índice único parcial `WHERE status<>'failed'` rejeitar o duplicado com 23505** — não usei `.upsert(onConflict)` porque supabase-js não consegue mirar índice parcial; efeito idêntico ao `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`), depois enqueue no sink; falha de enqueue/débito → `status='failed'` libera a chave p/ retry. Débito atômico via `deduct_mco_coins(p_user_id, p_amount)` fail-closed **antes** do send (dormente em Fatia 1: asset existente = 0 mco, trilho ligado p/ passo `generate` futuro).
- Também roteei o tick vivo `autopilot-cadence-cron` por `plan_kind` (`cadence`→`cadence-run` | `viral`→`autopilot-run`) — **nenhum job pg_cron novo** (FR-CAD-004) — e adicionei `[functions.cadence-run] verify_jwt=false` ao `config.toml`. (O router já estava sendo editado em paralelo — o roteamento por `plan_kind` está aplicado e type-checka.)

**Colunas de `autopilot_plans` que uso (p/ o crítico cruzar com `20260716120000_cadence_recurrence.sql` + base `20260620120000`):** `id, user_id, project_id, name, plan_kind, is_active, next_run_at, recurrence, quiet_hours, overlap_policy, program, channel_allowlist, budget_cap_mco`.

**Colunas de `cadence_dispatches` que escrevo/leio:** `id, user_id, plan_id, step_index, occurrence_at, idempotency_key, channel, status, target_ref` (INSERT status='queued' + UPDATE target_ref / status='failed'; leituras por `plan_id`/`user_id`/`channel`/`status`/`occurrence_at`). Não toco `external_usd_cost`/`cost_source`/`error` no dispatch (esses são do reconcile por webhook de status — FM-CAD-05; escrevo `error` só no path de falha).

**Divergências declaradas (Lei 1):**
1. **Sink `scheduled_posts`** — usei o contrato **VIVO** que o `auto-publish` realmente drena: `status:'queued'` + `scheduled_at` (não `publish_at`/`pending` da nomenclatura selada §7), espelhado de `reshape-pillar:487`. `metadata.reshape.content` (caption) + `source_asset_id` + marcador `cadence`; **nunca** ponho bucket/key no metadata (FMEA-011).
2. **TODO explícito (FR-CAD-006)** — um `creative_assets.id` cru **não tem branch resolver owner-scoped no `auto-publish`** (ele resolve `channel_variant_id`/`space_publish_variant_id`/`carousel_render_id`), então o dispatch enfileira **só a legenda**; ligar a imagem exige (a) branch no auto-publish que assine um `creative_assets.id` owner-scoped, ou (b) cunhar um `space_publish_variant`.
3. **TODO (FR-CAD-005)** — `sourceAssetId` é lido do `plan.program` jsonb (não existe coluna `source_asset_id` na migration); `cadence-plan` deve persistir lá (`{source_asset_id, steps}`).
4. **TODO (FR-CAD-007)** — `profiles.timezone` não é adicionada por nenhuma migration; leio defensivamente com fallback `America/Sao_Paulo`.
5. **A/B (FR-CAD-010)** e opt-out/jurisdição por-lead ficaram **fora** deste dispatch (canal broadcast não é lead-scoped; A/B depende de `creative_metrics.hook_rate` — OTD-CAD-016); os gates entregues são exatamente os 3 nomeados na diretiva + ownership + digest.

Não usei `begin/finalize` (nenhuma RPC `begin_cadence_cycle` é declarada na data-model — usei `deduct_mco_coins`, que existe, conforme a diretiva).
